## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear spine: section dividers (3, 14, 23, 34, 42, 54) set up each claim, and the pages after them carry it with one chart and a plain headline plus a one-line takeaway (6, 7, 15, 16, 17). The template is consistent, with a restrained grey and orange palette where orange marks the key series, and legible chart text with a source line on every data slide. Weaknesses remain: slides 4 and 5 have bland titles and no stated takeaway, slides 43 and 50 to 53 have thin or vague titles, 7's rotated year labels are crowded, and the abstract diagrams (22, 43, 49) are weak. Slide 9 is text with no visual, and slide 35 uses a foreign purple palette that is hard to read.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the one-chart, one-claim pattern with a headline, a subhead stating the takeaway and a source line (slides 6, 15, 16, 17), and the orange divider slides that mark the argument's sections.

**Most useful repair:** Give slides 4, 5, 50 to 53 and 43 action titles that state the point, and rebuild the weak diagram pages (22, 43, 49) so the structure is visible rather than loose text in chevrons.

**What would change the score:** I would recolour slide 35 into the deck palette, thin the year labels on slide 7, and use a big number or visual on slides 9, 32 and 40 instead of plain text.

_Confidence high. 54 of 54 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and a short framing before moving into its argument that mobile is driving technology's reach and scale. Early sections chart how smartphones bring more people online, using bar and line charts sourced from ITU, a16z and Ericsson, and then cover device costs and the growth of the mobile market. The middle sections argue that mobile has remade the tech industry, the internet and consumer electronics, with market share and unit-sales comparisons against PCs, cameras and games consoles. Later sections shift to how mobile dominates attention and how businesses are increasingly built around new technology, using Amazon and Wal-Mart as examples. The closing slides use Google Books frequency charts for railways, steel, computerization and software to argue that mature technology fades into the background, ending on the line that software is eating the world. Slides 4 to 7 and 14 to 21 are the most useful to study, as they show how a single chart is paired with one headline claim and a short source line.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Benedict Evans mobile deck have?**

The Benedict Evans deck has 54 slides. It opens with a title slide on slide 1 and closes with the line 'Software is eating the world' on slide 54.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about smartphone unit sales compared with PCs?**

Slide 15 states '4bn people buying phones every 2 years instead of 1.6bn buying PCs every 5 years.' The chart on that slide shows quarterly unit shipments for iOS and Android against PCs.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about Amazon's share of US retail?**

Slide 47 states that 'Amazon still only has 1.5% of US retail revenue.' The slide pairs this with a line chart of Amazon North America ecommerce as a percentage of US retail.

**What outlook does the Benedict Evans deck give for smartphone adoption by 2020?**

Slide 8 says 'By 2020 80% of adults on earth will have a smartphone.' The slide sources this to World Bank, GSMA and a16z.

Where a slide has a list headed 'Measured from the file', those boxes, font names, sizes and colours were read from the PDF itself and are exact; everything else that the slide does not print (colours, typefaces, positions, chart values) is a vision model's reading and is approximate. **Layout**

A single-column chart slide on a white page. The title sits top-left at x 19.1%, aligned left with the chart subtitle and y-axis start. A full-width light grey band (y 24.1% to 93.2%) holds the chart. The legend sits at the left edge of the band, and the source line is bottom-right at about 96% of the width. **Visuals**

Bar chart, clustered (grouped), vertical. Title: 'Billion people online'. Y-axis: 0 to 3 billion people, labelled 0, 1, 2, 3, with dotted horizontal gridlines at 1, 2 and 3 and a solid baseline at 0. Series 1, 'People online' (grey #666666): about 0.04 billion in 1995, about 0.4 billion in 2000, about 2.95 billion in 2014 (no values printed; these are estimates read from bar heights). Series 2, 'Smartphones' (orange #CE7C3B): only one bar, in 2014, about 2.0 billion (no value printed). Legend: top-left of the chart, vertical, square swatches with labels 'People online' (grey) and 'Smartphones' (orange). No highlighted points. The 2014 bars are the tallest and the smartphone bar is drawn beside the people-online bar.

**Design**

Background: Split background: white #FFFFFF at the top, with a light grey #F2F2F2 band from about 24% to 93% of the slide height. Clean, minimal, flat style. The top area is white #FFFFFF, and the chart band is light grey #F2F2F2. The title is near-black #101010 in a regular-weight geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova Regular), large, about 6.9% of slide height. The chart subtitle is semibold sans-serif in warm grey #7F7E7B, about 3.5% of height. Axis labels are about 2.5% of height, with category and legend labels in dark grey #3F3F3F and value and axis labels in mid grey #7F7E7B. Accent colours are grey #666666 and orange #CE7C3B. Gridlines are thin dotted grey #7F7E7B lines. Bars are solid with no outlines, corners or shadows.

### Slide 6

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A clean three-series-free bar chart with a headline that states the takeaway and a one-line subtitle, so the viewer reads the point before the data.
- Worth keeping: Keep the one-chart, one-claim pattern with a headline, a subhead stating the takeaway and a source line (slides 6, 15, 16, 17), and the orange divider slides that mark the argument's sections.

**Text**

Growth into the future - everyone
Another 1bn people will come online, all due to smartphones
Billion people online
5
People online
Smartphones
4
3
2
1
0
1995
2000
2014
2020
Source: ITU, a16z

**Layout**

Single-column slide with the title and subtitle left-aligned at x 19.1% on a white top band, and a full-width light-grey chart panel from y 24.1% to 93.2%. The chart legend sits at the left edge of the panel (x about 3%), the plot runs from x about 17% to 96%, and the source line is right-aligned at the bottom right of the white footer band. No footer, page number or logo is shown.

**Visuals**

Clustered vertical bar chart titled Billion people online. Y axis: Billion people, linear scale 0 to 5 with gridlines at each whole number, labels 0 1 2 3 4 5. X axis: years 1995, 2000, 2014, 2020, categorical. Series 1 People online, dark grey #666666: 1995 about 0.05, 2000 about 0.42, 2014 about 2.95, 2020 about 4.08. Series 2 Smartphones, orange #CE7C3B: 2014 about 2.0, 2020 about 4.08; no bars for 1995 or 2000. Legend at the left of the chart: dark grey square with People online, orange square with Smartphones. Gridlines are dotted mid-grey lines at each unit, and the zero baseline is a solid grey line. No bars are highlighted. Values are estimates from bar heights because no values are printed on the bars.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF with a full-width light grey #F2F2F2 band behind the chart from y 24.1% to 93.2%. Clean flat corporate infographic style. Background white #FFFFFF above and below a light grey #F2F2F2 chart panel. Title in Proxima Nova Regular, near-black #101010, large, about 6.9% of slide height. Subtitle in Proxima Nova Regular, black #000000, about 3.5% of slide height. Chart heading in Proxima Nova Semibold, grey #7F7E7B, about 3.5%. Axis numbers in Proxima Nova Regular, grey #7F7E7B, about 2.5%. Legend and year labels in Proxima Nova Regular, dark grey #3F3F3F, about 2.5%. Source line in Proxima Nova Regular, grey #7F7E7B, about 2.5%. Accent colours are dark grey #666666 and burnt orange #CE7C3B. Bars are flat rectangles with no outline, no shadow, no gradient and no rounded corners. Gridlines are dotted grey lines of hairline weight.

### Slide 7

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A minimal line chart that uses only two colours and direct labels at the line ends, which makes the crossover easy to read without a legend.
- What would change the score: I would recolour slide 35 into the deck palette, thin the year labels on slide 7, and use a big number or visual on slides 9, 32 and 40 instead of plain text.

**Text**

The end of the unconnected / Smartphones drive much greater internet penetration / Global adult population / 100% / No Internet / No smartphone / 75% / 50% / 25% / 0% / 1995 / 1996 / 1997 / 1998 / 1999 / 2000 / 2001 / 2002 / 2003 / 2004 / 2005 / 2006 / 2007 / 2008 / 2009 / 2010 / 2011 / 2012 / 2013 / 2014e / 2015e / 2016e / 2017e / 2018e / Source: a16z, World Bank, Apple, Google, Nokia

**Layout**

Single-column slide with a full-width white header band holding a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at about 19% of the slide width, which leaves the left edge blank above the chart. Below, a full-width light grey panel (from about 24% to 93% of the slide height) holds the chart, with the chart title left-aligned with the title at about 19% x and the legend at the far left at about 5% x. The source line is right-aligned at the bottom right under the grey panel, with no footer, logo or slide number visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled Global adult population. Y axis: percent of global adult population, 0% to 100%, gridlines and labels at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%. X axis: calendar years 1995 to 2018, labels 1995 to 2013 then 2014e, 2015e, 2016e, 2017e, 2018e (e = estimate), labels rotated about 45 degrees. Series 1, No Internet (orange line, about #D07A35, about 3 px stroke): about 99% in 1995, about 98% in 1996, about 97% in 1997, about 96% in 1998, about 94% in 1999, about 90% in 2000, about 88% in 2001, about 85% in 2002, about 82% in 2003, about 80% in 2004, about 78% in 2005, about 75% in 2006, about 71% in 2007, about 68% in 2008, about 64% in 2009, about 60% in 2010, about 56% in 2011, about 51% in 2012, about 48% in 2013, about 45% in 2014e, about 41% in 2015e, about 37% in 2016e, about 34% in 2017e. Series 2, No smartphone (dark grey line, about #666666, about 3 px stroke): about 100% in 2006, about 98% in 2007, about 96% in 2008, about 95% in 2009, about 92% in 2010, about 86% in 2011, about 78% in 2012, about 69% in 2013, about 57% in 2014e, about 48% in 2015e, about 40% in 2016e, about 35% in 2017e, about 30% in 2018e. The values are estimates read from the line positions, since no values are printed. Legend at left: orange line with No Internet, grey line with No smartphone, both stacked vertically. No highlighted points. Gridlines are dotted grey lines at each 25% step. The two series cross near 2017e.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF, with a light grey #F2F2F2 panel behind the chart area from about 24% to 93% of slide height. Clean minimal sans-serif look. Title in ProximaNova Regular at about 6.9% of slide height, near-black #101010. Subtitle in ProximaNova Regular at about 3.5% of slide height, black #000000. Chart title in ProximaNova Semibold at about 3.5%, warm grey #7F7E7B. Axis labels and source in ProximaNova Regular at about 2.5%, grey #7F7E7B for values and #3F3F3F for year labels and legend. Accents are the orange #D07A35 and dark grey #666666 series lines. Gridlines are thin dotted grey, the x axis baseline is a solid thin grey line. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 8

**Text**

The world in 2020 / By 2020 80% of adults on earth will have a smartphone / Global population (bn) / Growth to 2020 / 2014 / 8 / 7 / 6 / 5 / 4 / 3 / 2 / 1 / 0 / Population / Adults / Mobile / Online population / Smart phones / PCs / Source: World Bank, GSMA, a16z

**Layout**

Single-column slide with a left-aligned title and subtitle in the white top band, followed by a full-width light grey chart panel spanning the middle, with the chart's title and legend at its top-left and a right-aligned source line in the white bottom band. The title and subtitle start at about 19% of the slide width, the left margin of the chart title and plot; the legend sits at about 3% from the left edge. There is no footer or slide number.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical stacked column chart titled 'Global population (bn)'. Y-axis is labelled with integers 0 to 8 (unit: billions, bn), gridlines at each integer as dotted grey lines, no axis title. X-axis categories left to right: Population, Adults, Mobile, Online population, Smart phones, PCs. Two series stacked: 2014 (dark grey #666666, bottom segment) and Growth to 2020 (orange #CE7C3B, top segment). No values are printed on the bars; estimated values: Population about 7.0 (2014) plus about 0.4 growth, total about 7.4; Adults about 5.2 plus about 0.2, total about 5.4; Mobile about 3.6 plus about 0.7, total about 4.3; Online population about 2.7 plus about 1.3, total about 4.0; Smart phones about 2.0 plus about 2.0, total about 4.0; PCs about 1.6 (2014 only, no growth segment). Legend at top-left of the chart: orange square 'Growth to 2020' above dark grey square '2014'. No highlighted points. Bars are flat, solid, with no outlines and no data labels.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF top and bottom bands with a light grey #F2F2F2 panel behind the chart area. Clean, minimal flat design. Top band and bottom band are white #FFFFFF; the chart panel is light grey #F2F2F2 with no border. Title text is near-black #101010 in ProximaNova Regular (geometric sans-serif, regular weight), about 6.9% of slide height (cap height about 4.8%). Subtitle is black #000000 in ProximaNova Regular, about 3.5% of slide height. Chart title is grey #7F7E7B in ProximaNova Semibold, about 3.5%. Axis numerals are grey #7F7E7B in ProximaNova Regular, about 2.5%. Category labels and legend labels are dark grey #3F3F3F in ProximaNova Regular, about 2.5%. Source line is grey #7F7E7B in ProximaNova Regular, about 2.5%, right-aligned. Accent colours are orange #CE7C3B and dark grey #666666. Gridlines are dotted mid-grey lines. Shapes have square corners, no shadows, no gradients.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Large, sparse text on a plain background lets two numeric comparisons land as statements rather than data.
- Review: Slide 9 is text with no visual, and slide 35 uses a foreign purple palette that is hard to read.
- What would change the score: I would recolour slide 35 into the deck palette, thin the year labels on slide 7, and use a big number or visual on slides 9, 32 and 40 instead of plain text.

**Text**

An iPhone 6 CPU has 625 times more / transistors than a 1995 Pentium. / iPhone 6 launch weekend: Apple sold ~25x / more CPU transistors than were in all the / PCs on Earth in 1995. / Everyone gets a pocket supercomputer. / Source: Apple, Intel, a16z

**Layout**

A single left-aligned text block of body statements starts at about 19.5% of slide width and spans roughly 21.9% to 67.6% of slide height, with a blank line between the three paragraphs and the final statement; the block is not vertically centred and leaves a large empty margin below it. No title, no page number, no logo. A small source line sits at the bottom right, right-aligned to about 96% of slide width and about 96.9% of slide height.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid flat fill #F2F2F2 (light grey), with a #FFFFFF base fill beneath it covering the full slide. Flat light grey background #F2F2F2 with no gradient, pattern or texture. Body text is ProximaNova Light (geometric humanist sans-serif, light weight) in dark grey #3F3F3F at about 5.4% of slide height per line with generous line spacing (about 6.7% of slide height between baselines within a paragraph). No title styling, no accent colours, no lines, shapes, shadows or corner radii. The source line is ProximaNova Regular at about 2.5% of slide height in mid grey #7F7E7B.

**Layout**

Single-column chart slide. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 19% with the title in the upper band (y 6% to 12%) and the subtitle beneath it (y 15% to 18.5%). A full-width light grey panel (y 24% to 93%) holds the chart, with the chart title at its top-left, a vertical value axis with labels at the left of the plot, and six category labels under a horizontal baseline. The source line sits bottom-right on the white area below the panel, right-aligned to about x 96%. No slide number or logo is visible.

**Visuals**

Bar chart, vertical bars. Chart title: 'Sub-Saharan Africa population coverage'. Value axis: 0% to 80% in 10% steps, labelled at left, unit percent of population, gridlines dotted grey at each 10% step, baseline solid grey at 0%. Category axis: six categories. No values are printed on the bars, so estimates are given. Series, left to right: Cellular coverage, about 70%, dark grey #666666. 3G coverage now, about 20%, mid grey #666666 at 67% opacity (renders as lighter grey #929292). 3G coverage by 2019, about 65%, dark grey #666666. Improved water, about 60%, dark grey #666666. Electricity, about 32%, dark grey #666666. Mobile users, about 37%, orange #CE7C3B, the only highlighted bar. No legend. Bars are flat rectangles with no outline, all of equal width about 5% of slide width, evenly spaced.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF across the full slide, with a light grey #F2F2F2 band behind the chart from y 24.1% to 93.2%. White background. The chart panel is light grey #F2F2F2 with no border or shadow. Title text is near-black #101010, regular weight sans-serif (Proxima Nova Regular), about 6.9% of slide height. Subtitle is black #000000, regular sans-serif, about 3.5% of slide height. Chart title is semibold sans-serif in grey #7F7E7B, about 3.5% of slide height. Axis and value labels are regular sans-serif in grey #7F7E7B, about 2.5% of slide height. Category labels are regular sans-serif in dark grey #3F3F3F, about 2.5% of slide height. Source line is regular sans-serif in grey #7F7E7B, about 2.5% of slide height. Accent colour is orange #CE7C3B on one bar. Shapes are square-cornered with no gradients or shadows.

### Slide 12

**Text**

We go from this…
Source: James Cridland (https://www.flickr.com/photos/jamescridland/612782641)

**Layout**

Single-column layout on a white field. The title sits top-left, starting at about x 19% and y 6%, and the photograph is set below it, left-aligned to about x 18.5%. The source line sits under the photograph's lower-right corner and is right-aligned to about x 96%. There is no footer, page number or logo.

**Visuals**

One photograph, boxed as a full-width rectangle with no crop, rounded corners, shadow or overlay. Subject: a very large outdoor stadium concert at night, seen from an elevated viewpoint. Tens of thousands of spectators fill the tiers and floor, and many hold up illuminated mobile phones, giving scattered blue-white points of light across the crowd. The stage is at lower centre, with a bright white-lit structure and a tall lighting truss or crane rising at left-centre. A red-lit roof canopy with angled spikes runs across the top edge. Composition: wide landscape frame, crowd filling most of the area, sky a dark grey-black strip at the top. Lighting: night, artificial, mixed red stage and roof wash with cool white-blue phone lights. Dominant colours: crimson red, magenta, purple-white specks, near-black shadows. The photograph is the only visual element.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background (#FFFFFF). Title in ProximaNova-Regular, regular weight, sentence case, colour #101010, about 6.9% of slide height, left-aligned. Source line in ProximaNova-Regular, regular weight, about 2.5% of slide height, colour #7F7E7B, right-aligned. Sans-serif geometric typeface. No accent colours, rules or decorative shapes; the only colour comes from the photograph. The slide has two stacked full-slide #FFFFFF fills at 0,0 100x100.

### Slide 13

**Text**

… To this
Source: Alan Knott-Craig

**Layout**

Single column on a plain white slide. The title '… To this' sits top-left, starting at about 19% from the left edge and about 6% from the top. A landscape photograph spans most of the width below it, starting at about 3.5% from the left and running almost to the bottom edge. The source credit is right-aligned on the bottom line, flush with the photo's right edge. There is no footer, page number or logo.

**Visuals**

One photograph, a landscape rectangle with sharp square corners and no border or frame. Subject: a man in a purple zip-up track jacket with orange and white trim, dark green trousers and black boots, wearing a white cap and holding a mobile phone in both hands at chest height while looking down at it. He stands beside a rendered mud house with a thatched dark-brown roof, a turquoise-painted wall and an open black doorway to his left. To his right is a sloped bank of bare orange-ochre earth with green grass above it. Lighting is hard, bright sunlight from the front left, casting a long shadow of the man onto the ground to his right and a shadow on the wall. Dominant colours are turquoise, ochre, green, purple and dark thatch brown. Framing is a wide shot with the man near the centre-right. Nothing is cropped at the edges, and there is no overlay or tint. The photo's visible area is about x 19.2, y 27.6, w 76.5, h 36.5 of the slide.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background (#FFFFFF). Title in regular-weight humanist sans-serif (ProximaNova-Regular), colour #101010, roughly 6.9% of slide height, left-aligned. Source credit in the same family, regular weight, colour #7F7E7B, roughly 2.5% of slide height, right-aligned. No accent colours, rules, shapes or shadows. Photo has square corners and no shadow.

### Slide 15

**Review notes**

- Worth keeping: Keep the one-chart, one-claim pattern with a headline, a subhead stating the takeaway and a source line (slides 6, 15, 16, 17), and the orange divider slides that mark the argument's sections.

**Text**

One line chart. Type: line chart with a single series. Title: 'Smartphone & tablet share of global CE retail revenue' (printed above the plot). ### Slide 18

**Text**

Shift away from Microsoft / Microsoft’s near-absence from mobile has ended its dominance / Microsoft share of personal computing device sales* / 100% / 75% / 50% / 25% / 0% / Mar-09 / Sep-09 / Mar-10 / Sep-10 / Mar-11 / Sep-11 / Mar-12 / Sep-12 / Mar-13 / Sep-13 / Mar-14 / Sep-14 / * Smartphones, tablets, PCs & Macs. Source: Gartner, Apple, Google, Microsoft, Nokia, Blackberry, a16z

**Layout**

Single-column chart slide on a 16:9 canvas. The title and subtitle sit top-left, left-aligned at x 19.1%, with a white header band above a full-width light grey chart panel spanning y 24.1% to 93.2%. The chart plot runs from about x 19.4% to 96.0%, with the y-axis labels to its left and the x-axis labels beneath. The footnote sits bottom-right, right-aligned to about x 96%, below the panel on white. No page number or logo is visible.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Type: line chart with a single series. Chart title: 'Microsoft share of personal computing device sales*' (semibold, grey). Footnote: '* Smartphones, tablets, PCs & Macs. Source: Gartner, Apple, Google, Microsoft, Nokia, Blackberry, a16z'. Y-axis: Microsoft share, percent, range 0% to 100%, labelled at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X-axis: time, semi-annual, first label Mar-09 and last label Sep-14, labels at Mar-09, Sep-09, Mar-10, Sep-10, Mar-11, Sep-11, Mar-12, Sep-12, Mar-13, Sep-13, Mar-14, Sep-14, with a solid grey baseline at 0%. The series is a single orange line (about #CD7A35), stroke about 0.5% of slide height thick, with 24 vertices and no markers. No values are printed; estimated values, marked 'about', for the labelled dates: Mar-09 about 91%, Sep-09 about 82%, Mar-10 about 82%, Sep-10 about 67%, Mar-11 about 56%, Sep-11 about 49%, Mar-12 about 34%, Sep-12 about 29%, Mar-13 about 22%, Sep-13 about 21%, Mar-14 about 18%, Sep-14 about 17%, final point about 16%. Legend: none. Highlighted points: none; the line ends at about 16% at the right edge.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF across the full slide, with a light grey #F2F2F2 panel from y 24.1% to 93.2% behind the chart. Clean minimal corporate look. Background is white with a light grey #F2F2F2 panel behind the chart area only. Title text is near-black #101010, subtitle is black #000000, chart title is grey #7F7E7B in semibold, axis labels grey #7F7E7B for the percentages and dark grey #3F3F3F for the dates, footnote grey #7F7E7B. Accent colour is the orange data line, about #CD7A35. Typeface is Proxima Nova (geometric humanist sans-serif): Regular for title, subtitle, axis labels and footnote, Semibold for chart title. Title size about 6.9% of slide height, subtitle and chart title about 3.5%, axis and footnote labels about 2.5%. Gridlines are thin dotted grey lines; the baseline is a solid grey line. No shadows, gradients, or corner radii.

### Slide 19

**Text**

China is the biggest smartphone market / Historic dominance starting to shift / Million people / Chinese mobile / internet users / EU population / USA population / 600 / 500 / 400 / 300 / 200 / 100 / 0 / 2007 / 2008 / 2009 / 2010 / 2011 / 2012 / 2013 / 2014 / Source: CNNIC, US Census Bureau, Eurostat

**Layout**

Two-band layout on a full-width slide. A white header band holds a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at x 19.1%, with the title at the top (y 5.7%) and the subtitle beneath it. A full-width light grey panel (y 24.1% to 93.2%) holds the chart, whose unit label, legend and y-axis labels start at the same x 19.1% line as the title. The chart sits inside the panel with the legend at the far left (x 5%). The source line is right-aligned to about x 96% in the white footer strip below the panel. No slide number or logo is visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled by the caption 'Million people'. Y-axis: 0 to 600 in steps of 100, unit million people, dotted horizontal gridlines at each 100 step, solid grey baseline at 0. X-axis: years 2007 to 2014 with labels at each year, points plotted at roughly half-year intervals. Series 1, 'Chinese mobile internet users', orange line rising from about 50 (2007) to about 62 (2007.25), about 72 (2007.5), about 92 (2007.75), about 112 (2008), about 135 (2008.25), about 155 (2008.5), about 200 (2008.75), about 233 (2009), about 278 (2009.5), about 303 (2010), about 320 (2010.5), about 355 (2011), about 388 (2011.5), about 418 (2012), about 462 (2012.5), about 500 (2013), about 527 (2013.5) and about 556 (2014); no values are printed, all are estimates. Series 2, 'EU population', dark grey line nearly flat at about 497 in 2007, about 503 from 2008.75 to 2013, and about 507 to 2014; estimates. Series 3, 'USA population', light blue-grey line at about 302 in 2007, about 310 from 2008.75, about 315 from 2011, about 318 from 2013 and about 320 in 2014; estimates. Legend at left: three short thick line swatches with labels in the same order as the series. No highlighted points; the orange line crosses the EU line near 2013 and the USA line near 2010.5. Lines are thick (about 0.5% of slide height) with rounded joins and no markers.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF header and footer bands behind a light grey #F2F2F2 chart panel.

Segments are separated by small gaps and the iPhone segment is offset slightly outward. No axes, no legend; each segment carries its label outside the ring. Segment colours clockwise from the top: iPhone dark grey #666666 (starts at top-right, spanning roughly 12 o'clock to 4 o'clock), Xiaomi orange #CE7C3B (about 4 to 6 o'clock), MEIZU pale blue-grey #C9D3E1 (about 6 to 7:30), Samsung steel blue #708CB0 (about 7:30 to 8:30), Huawei slate grey #4F6A6C (about 8:30 to 9), Other dark navy #093E58 (about 9 to 12). No segments are highlighted beyond the iPhone segment being pulled out. The ring is open-centred with no centre label.

**Layout**

Title sits top-left in the white header band, left-aligned at about 19% of slide width with two lines of text. The grid's right edge sits at about 96% of width; there is no footer, slide number, or logo.

**Visuals**

Table with 2 data columns (Old, New) and 3 rows (Computing platform, Chips, Mobile), plus a row-label column. Row 'Computing platform': Old = Microsoft; New = Apple & Google (cell highlighted in orange #CE7C3B, the only highlighted cell). Row 'Chips': Old = Intel; New = ARM, Qualcomm, Mediatek (wrapped over two lines as 'ARM, Qualcomm,' / 'Mediatek'). Row 'Mobile': Old = Nokia & DoCoMo; New = China, San Francisco. Cells are flat grey #A6A6A6 rectangles separated by thin white gutters of about 0.3% of slide height; the table sits on a #F2F2F2 band. No photographs, icons, or charts; no legend.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF background, with a light grey #F2F2F2 band across the middle of the slide. White #FFFFFF background with a full-width light grey #F2F2F2 band behind the table. Title text in near-black #101010, regular-weight geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Regular style), about 6.9% of slide height per line with generous line spacing. Row labels and column headers in dark grey #3F3F3F, light-weight sans-serif (ProximaNova-Light style), about 4.4% of slide height. Table cell text in white #FFFFFF, light sans-serif at about 4.4% of slide height, centred. Accent colour is burnt orange #CE7C3B used for the single highlighted cell. Cell fills are flat #A6A6A6 with no shadows, gradients, or corner radii; the cells are sharp-cornered rectangles.

### Slide 22

**Text**

Mobile supply chain dominates all tech
The size of the smartphone business means it is driving into every other part of tech
Flood of smartphone / components – Lego for / technology
Augmented Reality, / Virtual Reality, / Wearables, Internet of / Things, connected cars, / connected home, / drones etc
Hardware tech for / almost anything is on / the shelf
Contract manufacturers / can assemble those / components into / anything
The challenge is vision / and route to market

**Layout**

Title is set left-of-centre at the top, starting around 19% of slide width, with a one-line subtitle beneath it at the same left edge. Below, three chevron-bounded panels run full-bleed across the slide width from about 24% to 93% of slide height, with the left panel's text at the left margin (about 3%) and the middle and right panel texts indented to about 38% and 73%; no footer or slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

A three-stage horizontal process band made of three adjacent light grey panels separated by two chevron-shaped dashed borders. The left panel has an orange dashed outline (about #C87A3C) on its top, left and right-chevron edges; the boundary between the middle and right panels and the top and bottom edges of the middle and right panels use a slate grey dashed line (about #5A6670). The chevron points are at about 35.7% and 73% of slide width, pointing right. No photographs, icons, logos or charts are present.

**Design**

Background: Solid white (#FFFFFF) background, with a full-slide #F2F2F2 grey band behind the central process panels. Source: IBM, a16z

**Layout**

Two-zone slide: a white header band holds a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at about 19% of the slide width, and a light grey full-width panel holds the chart from about 24% to 93% of the slide height. The legend sits at the far left of the panel, the y-axis labels sit in a column left of the plot, and the source line is right-aligned in the white footer band at the bottom. There is no slide number visible.

**Visuals**

Chart: grouped column chart titled 'Mobile share of 'Black Friday' ecommerce'. Y-axis: share of ecommerce, percent, range 0% to 60% in 10% steps, labels 0%, 10%, 20%, 30%, 40%, 50%, 60%. X-axis: years 2012, 2013, 2014 as categories. Series 1 'Traffic' in dark grey #666666: about 24% (2012), about 39% (2013), about 49% (2014). Series 2 'Value' in orange #CE7C3B: about 15% (2012), about 22% (2013), about 28% (2014). No data labels are printed, so all bar values are estimates marked 'about'. Legend at left: square swatch #666666 labelled Traffic above square swatch #CE7C3B labelled Value. No highlighted points. Gridlines are dotted mid-grey lines at each 10% step; the 0% baseline is a solid grey line.

**Design**

Background white #FFFFFF for the header and footer, with a light grey panel #F2F2F2 behind the chart. Title in near-black #101010, subtitle in black #000000, chart title in grey #7F7E7B semibold, axis labels in grey #7F7E7B, category labels and legend labels in dark grey #3F3F3F. Accent colours are dark grey #666666 and orange #CE7C3B. Typeface is Proxima Nova (geometric humanist sans-serif): Regular for title, subtitle, labels and source; Semibold for chart title. Title text is about 6.9% of slide height, subtitle and chart title about 3.5%, labels and source about 2.5%. Bars are flat with no outlines, shadows or rounded corners; gridlines are dotted.

66%

16%

1%

9%
Source: Ofcom

**Layout**

A single centred column of content on a white slide. The title and subtitle are centred across the top, above a full-width light grey panel that holds the chart title and a donut chart. The donut sits left of centre inside the panel, with its labels to the left and right of the ring. The source line is right-aligned in the white band below the panel. There is no footer and no slide number.

**Visuals**

One donut chart with no axes or legend. Chart title: 'Where do you use the internet on your phone? (UK, Q1 2014)'. Segments, clockwise from the top: 'Both at home and outside' 66% in pale blue-grey #C9D3E1, large and unexploded, running from about 12 o'clock round the left side to about 6 o'clock; 'Mainly at home' 16% in orange #CE7C3B, from about 6 o'clock to about 4 o'clock, pulled slightly outward; 'Only at home' 8% in dark grey #666666, pulled outward; 'Only out of home' 1% in near-black #1E1E1E, a very thin exploded sliver (colour about, not printed); 'Mainly out of home' 9% in slate teal #50676E, pulled outward at about 1 o'clock. The gaps between the exploded segments are about 1 percent of the slide width. Every segment has its label and percentage printed beside it, so no legend is used. No axes, gridlines or highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF across the whole slide, with a #F2F2F2 light grey panel across the lower-middle band. Flat, minimal style. Background white #FFFFFF with a light grey panel #F2F2F2. Title in ProximaNova-Regular, a geometric humanist sans-serif, near-black #101010, about 6.9% of slide height, normal weight, centred. Subtitle in ProximaNova-Regular, black #000000, about 3.5% of slide height, centred. Chart title in ProximaNova-Semibold, grey #7F7E7B, about 3.5% of slide height, centred. Segment labels in ProximaNova-Regular, dark grey #4B4B4B, about 2.5% of slide height, centred over two lines. Source line in ProximaNova-Regular, grey #7F7E7B, about 2.5% of slide height, right-aligned. Shapes have no outlines, shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 28

**Text**

Platforms are more complex / Ecosystem dynamics depend on where you are and what you’re doing / Mobile platform share, Q4 2014 / 100% / Android / iOS / 75% / 50% / 25% / 0% / Phone unit sales / Global Browsing / Delhi FB users / SF FB users / App store revenue / Source: Facebook, Apple, Google, Gartner, Akamai, a16z

**Layout**

Title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 19% on a white top band, starting at the same left edge as the chart heading. A full-width light grey chart panel spans from about y 24% to y 93%, with a stacked column chart inside, a legend at the far left (x 3.5%), and the source line right-aligned at the bottom right (ending about x 96%) below the panel. No footer bar and no slide number are visible.

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart, titled 'Mobile platform share, Q4 2014'. Y-axis: 0% to 100% in 25% steps, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%. X-axis categories: Phone unit sales, Global Browsing, Delhi FB users, SF FB users, App store revenue. Series: iOS (dark grey #666666, bottom segment) and Android (orange #CE7C3B, top segment). Values are not printed; estimated from bar heights: Phone unit sales iOS about 10%, Android about 50% (total about 60%); Global Browsing iOS about 50%, Android about 40% (total about 90%); Delhi FB users iOS about 7%, Android about 62% (total about 69%); SF FB users iOS about 58%, Android about 33% (total about 91%); App store revenue iOS about 67%, Android about 33% (total 100%). Legend at top left: orange square labelled Android above dark grey square labelled iOS. No highlighted points. Bars are evenly spaced, roughly 120 px wide each at slide width 2000 px. Baseline is a solid grey axis line at 0%.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF with a full-width light grey #F2F2F2 band behind the chart area. Light grey panel #F2F2F2 behind the chart on a white slide. Title in ProximaNova Regular, near-black #101010, large sans-serif, about 6.9% of slide height for cap-height-including text. Subtitle in ProximaNova Regular, black #000000, about 3.5% of slide height. Chart heading in ProximaNova Semibold, grey #7F7E7B, about 3.5%. Axis labels and source in ProximaNova Regular, grey #7F7E7B for values and source, dark grey #3F3F3F for category labels and legend, about 2.5% of slide height. Series colours: iOS #666666, Android #CE7C3B. Gridlines are dotted grey. Bars are flat with no corner radius, shadow or gradient.

### Slide 29

**Text**

Phones are more sophisticated than PCs
Proliferation of sensors creates far more capability and sophistication than PCs
A computer shouldn’t ask anything that it should know
Sensors profoundly change what a computer can know
Every new sensor creates a new business

**Layout**

Title and subtitle are left-aligned at the same left edge (about 19.1% of slide width) in the upper white band, with the title above the subtitle. Below, a full-width light grey band (y 24.1% to 93.2%) holds three equal circles in a row joined by two block arrows, horizontally offset to the right of the slide's left margin; the band sits on a white page with no footer or slide number.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles connected left to right by two solid block arrows. Arrow 2: right-pointing solid block arrow, fill #CE7B3B, at x 70.4%, y 54.0%, about 4.5% by 9.3%, pointing from circle 2 to circle 3. Circle 3: mid grey #7F7F7F, about 20.4% by 36.2%, at x 76.7%, y 40.5%, containing centred white text 'Every new / sensor / creates a / new / business'. No photographs, logos, icons or charts.

Shapes are flat with no outlines, no shadows, no gradients and no rounded corners beyond perfect circles; arrows are solid block arrows with a notched chevron head. **Text**

Facebook’s mobile ad explosion / Mobile ads are now 68% of revenue and brought in $7.4bn in 2014 / Facebook revenue by source ($bn) / 4 / Mobile ads / Payments & Other / 3 / Desktop ads / 2 / 1 / 0 / Jun-11 Sep-11 Dec-11 Mar-12 Jun-12 Sep-12 Dec-12 Mar-13 Jun-13 Sep-13 Dec-13 Mar-14 Jun-14 Sep-14 Dec-14 Mar-15 / Source: Facebook, a16z

**Layout**

Full-width white header area with the title left-aligned at about x 19% and the subtitle beneath it at the same left edge. A light grey (#F2F2F2) chart panel spans the full slide width from about 24% to 93% of the height, with the chart title at its top-left, a legend column at the far left (x about 3%), the stacked area chart occupying x 19% to 96%, and the source line bottom-right on white at about x 82%, y 94%.

**Visuals**

Stacked area chart titled 'Facebook revenue by source ($bn)'. Y-axis: $bn from 0 to 4, gridlines dotted at 1, 2, 3, 4, labels 0 to 4 at left of plot. X-axis: quarterly time points from Jun-11 to Mar-15 (16 points, labels every quarter shown as Jun-11, Sep-11, Dec-11, Mar-12, Jun-12, Sep-12, Dec-12, Mar-13, Jun-13, Sep-13, Dec-13, Mar-14, Jun-14, Sep-14, Dec-14, Mar-15). Three stacked series from bottom to top: Desktop ads (dark grey #666666, about 0.78 at Jun-11, about 0.93 at Sep-12, about 0.88 at Mar-13, about 1.08 at Dec-13, about 1.00 at Jun-14, about 1.11 at Dec-14, about 0.88 at Mar-15); Payments & Other (light blue-grey #C9D3E1, thin band about 0.1 to 0.2 thick throughout); Mobile ads (orange #CE7C3B, about 0 at Jun-11 rising to about 1.25 at Sep-12, about 2.0 at Sep-13, about 2.57 at Dec-13, about 2.9 at Jun-14, about 3.83 at Dec-14, about 3.53 at Mar-15 as top of stack). Legend at left, top to bottom: Mobile ads (orange square), Payments & Other (light blue-grey square), Desktop ads (dark grey square). No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF with a full-slide grey #F2F2F2 chart panel from y 24.1% to 93.2%. White background with a light grey #F2F2F2 chart panel. Title in ProximaNova Regular, near-black #101010, large (about 6.9% of slide height, sentence case). Subtitle in ProximaNova Regular, black #000000, about 3.5% of slide height. Chart title in ProximaNova Semibold, grey #7F7E7B, about 3.5%. Axis labels in ProximaNova Regular, grey #7F7E7B for numbers and dark grey #3F3F3F for category and legend labels, about 2.5%. Source line in ProximaNova Regular, grey #7F7E7B, about 2.5%, right-aligned. Gridlines are thin dotted grey lines. Areas are flat with no shadows or gradients.

WhatsApp: 30bn messages a day.
(with just 40 engineers)

**Layout**

A single left-aligned text block of three lines starts at about 19.5% from the left edge and 22% from the top, with generous blank space to the right and below. The three lines are separated by equal vertical gaps of about 13% of slide height. The source line sits in the bottom-right corner, right-aligned to about 96% of slide width. There is no title, no slide number and no footer bar.

none

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 fill covering the full slide, over a white #FFFFFF base. Flat light-grey background #F2F2F2 with no gradient, texture or border. Body text is set in a light geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova Light) in dark grey #3F3F3F at about 5.4% of slide height, with no bold or italics. The source line is Proxima Nova Regular in mid grey #7F7E7B at about 2.5% of slide height. No accent colours, shapes or lines are used.

### Slide 35

**Review notes**

- Review: Slide 9 is text with no visual, and slide 35 uses a foreign purple palette that is hard to read.
- What would change the score: I would recolour slide 35 into the deck palette, thin the year labels on slide 7, and use a big number or visual on slides 9, 32 and 40 instead of plain text.

**Layout**

Left-aligned title block sits in the upper area starting at about 19% from the left, with the title above a one-line subtitle, both sharing the same left edge. A wide chart occupies the lower two-thirds, sitting on a full-width light grey band, and the source line is right-aligned at the bottom right beneath the chart; there is no footer or slide number.

**Visuals**

Chart: 100% stacked area chart, untitled, showing the share of time awake spent on activities across the day. Y axis: 0% to 100% in 10% steps, labelled on the left. X axis: clock time from 06:00 to 05:46 in 23-minute steps, tick labels printed vertically in small bold purple text. Series (bottom to top): 'Any media and communications activity' in dark purple #652266, rising from about 4% at 06:00 to about 40% by 08:00, about 43-45% through the daytime, peaking at about 73% around 21:20, then falling to about 50% at 22:52, about 33% at 23:15 and under 5% by about 01:30; 'Other activities (Time awake but not undertaking media/ comms activities)' in lime green #A4CE3A, the band above the purple; 'Sleep' in light grey #E8E8E8, the top band at the start and end of the day, reaching about 93% at 06:00 and falling to about 50% by 08:41, with the sleep share rising again after about 22:00 to near 100% by 02:00 and about 94% at 05:46. No printed numeric values other than axis ticks; the values above are estimates. Four thin pale lavender vertical divider lines (#D9B8E0, about 1 px) run from the top to the baseline at about 08:41, 17:30, 22:52 and 00:24. Band labels beneath the axis: Breakfast time (6:00am to 8.59am) spanning the first divider, Day time (9:00am to 5.30pm), Peak (5:30pm to 10.59pm), Post peak (11:00pm to 12.29am), Night time (12:30pm to 5.59am), in bold purple titles with regular purple times. No legend; labels are placed directly on the areas. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF, with a full-width light grey band #F2F2F2 behind the chart area. White slide with a light grey band (#F2F2F2) behind the chart area. Title in ProximaNova Regular sans-serif, near-black #101010, at about 6.9% of slide height; subtitle in ProximaNova Regular, black #000000, about 3.5% of slide height. Source line in ProximaNova Regular, grey #7F7E7B, about 2.5%. Chart palette: purple #652266, lime green #A4CE3A, light grey #E8E8E8, lavender lines. Shapes are flat with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Chart title: Global LCD screen sales (bn square feet). Type: single-series line chart with straight segments and a slightly rounded look at the joints. Background white (#FFFFFF) with a light grey chart panel (#F2F2F2) spanning full width behind the chart. Title in regular-weight geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova), near-black #101010, large (about 6.9% of slide height for cap-height line). ### Slide 37

**Text**

TV sets are in the minority / Computing devices used for video now far outnumber actual ‘televisions’ / Global install base of video players, 2014 / TVs / 24% / PCs & Macs / 30% / WP/RIM / 1% / iOS / 13% / Google Android / 23% / Chinese Android / 9% / “By the summer of 2012, / the majority of the / televisions you see in / stores will have Google / TV embedded” / - Eric Schmidt / Source: Apple, Google, Nokia, Blackberry, Displaysearch, a16z

**Layout**

Title and subtitle are left-aligned at the same left edge (about x 19.1%) in the top white band, with the subtitle below the title. A full-width light grey panel spans from y 24.1% to y 93.2%, holding a donut chart on the left (about x 12% to 53%) and a quotation block on the right starting at x 64.7%. The source line sits bottom-right in the white band below the panel, right-aligned to about x 96%. No slide number or footer logo is visible.

**Visuals**

Donut chart titled 'Global install base of video players, 2014', semibold grey text above the ring. Five-plus segments with labelled values, clockwise from top: TVs 24% (dark grey #666666, slightly offset/exploded outward at top), iOS 13% (orange #CE7C3B, exploded slightly), Google Android 23% (pale blue-grey #C9D3E1), Chinese Android 9% (slate #50676E), WP/RIM 1% (near-black #222222, thin sliver), PCs & Macs 30% (dark teal-navy #093E58, starts at top after the gap and runs counter-clockwise). Labels sit outside the ring with thin leader lines connecting to each segment, except Google Android and its 23% label which sit inside the ring's lower opening. No axes, no legend, no highlighted points beyond the exploded segments. Ring thickness is about one-quarter of the outer radius; a small gap separates TVs from iOS and PCs from TVs. No photograph or logo.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF band at top and bottom, with a light grey #F2F2F2 panel behind the chart and quote from y 24.1% to y 93.2%.

**Layout**

Two-part slide on a white ground. A large title and a smaller subtitle sit in the upper area, starting at about 19% from the left edge, with the title near the top at about 6% and the subtitle at about 15%. A full-width light grey (#F2F2F2) chart panel spans from about 24% to 93% of the height, with the chart title at its top-left, a legend column at the left, and a stacked column chart filling the rest. The source line sits right-aligned in the white band below the panel at about 94% of the height, with no slide number visible.

**Visuals**

Chart: 100% stacked column chart titled 'Media you would miss most', UK aged 11-15, 2014. Two columns, Boys (left, x about 31-46%) and Girls (right, x about 69-84%). Y-axis runs 0% to 100% in 25% steps, labelled 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each step. X-axis categories: Boys, Girls. Series, stacked bottom to top: Mobile & tablet (#CE7C3B orange), PC (#666666 grey), Games console (#3F3F3F near-black), TV set (#C9D3E1 pale blue-grey), Other (#50676E dark slate teal). No data values are printed on the chart, so the values are estimates: Boys is about 38% Mobile & tablet, about 18% PC, about 21% Games console, about 18% TV set, about 5% Other. Girls is about 64% Mobile & tablet, about 10% PC, about 3% Games console, about 18% TV set, about 5% Other. Legend at the left, listed top to bottom: Other, TV set, Games console, PC, Mobile & tablet, each with a small coloured square of the series colour. No highlighted points. Flat fills, no shadows or gradients.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF, with a light grey #F2F2F2 panel covering the chart area from about 24% to 93% of the height. Plain white background with a light grey (#F2F2F2) chart panel. Title and subtitle in near-black (#101010 title, #000000 subtitle). Chart title in mid grey #7F7E7B, semibold. Axis labels in #7F7E7B, legend labels and category labels in #3F3F3F. Typeface is Proxima Nova (geometric sans-serif); title and subtitle Regular, chart title Semibold. Title is about 6.9% of slide height, subtitle and chart title about 3.5%, axis, legend and source text about 2.5%. Flat style: no borders on bars, thin 1px grey baseline at 0%, dotted grey gridlines, no corner radii or shadows.

No data labels are printed; values are estimates. Adults: Email about 33%, Phone calls about 20%, Social networks about 18%, Messaging apps about 26%, Photo messages about 3%. 11 to 15: Email about 2%, Phone calls about 3%, Social networks about 37%, Messaging apps about 48%, Photo messages about 10%. Legend at the left, listed top to bottom: Photo messages, Messaging apps, Social networks, Phone calls, Email, each with a small square swatch in the series colour. No highlighted points. Bars are flat-filled with no outlines, roughly 15% of slide width each, and the category axis line is mid-grey.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Japanese camera unit sales (m)'. Y-axis: unit sales in millions, linear scale 0 to 120, labelled at 0, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100, 120, with dotted grey horizontal gridlines at each label. X-axis: years 1951 to 2013 labelled every two years, rotated vertically, with a solid grey baseline at 0. Three series: Film (orange #C87A3A line), Digital, DSLR etc (dark grey #666666 line), and Digital, Fixed lens (light blue-grey #A9B8CC line). Film: about 0.3 in 1951, about 7 in 1976, about 17 around 1981, peak about 37 in 1997, falling to about 10 in 2005 and about 0 by 2008 where the line ends. Digital, DSLR etc: starts about 0 in 2002, about 2 in 2004, about 10 in 2007, peaks about 21 in 2011, and falls to about 13 in 2014. Digital, Fixed lens: starts about 0 in 1998, about 14 in 2002, about 57 in 2004, about 61 in 2005, about 74 in 2006, peaks about 110 in 2008, dips to about 96 in 2009, about 108 in 2010, about 99 in 2011, and falls to about 46 in 2013 and about 30 in 2014. Legend: vertical list at left with line swatches and labels in the order Film, Digital DSLR etc, Digital Fixed lens. No values are printed on the chart, so all values are estimates.

### Slide 43

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A three-part list slide that builds one concept step by step with simple, parallel phrasing.
- Review: Weaknesses remain: slides 4 and 5 have bland titles and no stated takeaway, slides 43 and 50 to 53 have thin or vague titles, 7's rotated year labels are crowded, and the abstract diagrams (22, 43, 49) are weak.
- Suggested repair: Give slides 4, 5, 50 to 53 and 43 action titles that state the point, and rebuild the weak diagram pages (22, 43, 49) so the structure is visible rather than loose text in chevrons.

**Text**

Three phases of technology deployment
Companies that make / technology
Companies that buy / technology
Companies that are / built around technology

**Layout**

Title sits top-centre-right on a white field, left edge of title at about 19% of width. Three chevron-shaped panels run edge to edge horizontally across the middle band of the slide, starting at about 24% of height and ending at about 93%, each label left-aligned near the left of its panel with no footer or slide number visible.

### Slide 44

**Text**

Companies that buy technology
Tech has become part of every office
Like plants, tech is part of facilities

**Layout**

Single title line across the top left-of-centre, aligned to a left edge at about 19% of the slide width. Below it, a potted plant photograph sits on the left half (about 19% to 61% of width, 22% to 97% of height) and a three-paragraph text block is right-aligned to a left edge at 64.7% of width, vertically centred around the middle of the slide. No footer and no slide number are visible.

**Visuals**

One photograph of a potted plant: a dracaena-type houseplant with long, upright, strap-like green leaves with pale cream/white longitudinal striping and some yellow-tipped leaf edges, growing from a terracotta-orange plastic nursery pot with a rolled rim and small dark drainage feet. The plant is shot straight-on against a pure white background, so it appears cut out with no visible background. Composition is centred on the pot, full plant visible from the pot base to the top leaves, lit evenly with soft light and no visible shadow. Dominant colours: several greens (#2E6B2F to #7FA86A), terracotta orange (#B5543A) for the pot. The image is not cropped, not full-bleed, and has no overlay or tint; it sits unboxed on the white slide.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradients, shadows or decorative shapes. Title in ProximaNova-Regular, a geometric humanist sans-serif, regular weight, black #101010, roughly 6.9% of slide height (cap height about 4.8%). Body text in ProximaNova-Light, a light-weight geometric sans-serif, dark grey #3F3F3F, about 4.4% of slide height, with generous paragraph spacing between the three statements. No accent colours, rules or borders. Minimal, uncluttered corporate style.

### Slide 48

**Text**

Amazon versus Wal-Mart / (Why would you stop to take profits?) / Quarterly revenue ($bn) / 140 / Amazon / Wal-Mart / 120 / 100 / 80 / 60 / 40 / 20 / 0 / 1989 / 1991 / 1993 / 1995 / 1997 / 1999 / 2001 / 2003 / 2005 / 2007 / 2009 / 2011 / 2013 / Source: Bloomberg

**Layout**

Single-column layout on a white slide. The title sits top-left at about 19% from the left edge, with the subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned on the same left edge. A full-width light grey band occupies the middle of the slide from about 24% to 93% of the height and holds the chart heading, legend at the left and the line chart filling the right. The source line sits bottom-right on the white area below the band, right-aligned to about 96% of the width. There is no footer and no slide number.

**Visuals**

One line chart titled Quarterly revenue ($bn), with a legend at left listing Amazon in orange and Wal-Mart in dark grey. Y axis: revenue in $bn, linear, range 0 to 140, gridlines and labels every 20 (0, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100, 120, 140), drawn as dotted grey horizontal lines. X axis: calendar years labelled every two years from 1989 to 2013 (1989, 1991, 1993, 1995, 1997, 1999, 2001, 2003, 2005, 2007, 2009, 2011, 2013), with the series running on to about 2014 at the right edge. Series Wal-Mart (dark grey line, about 2.5 px stroke): starts about 5 in 1989, rises slowly to about 20 by 1995, about 40 by 1999, about 60 by 2002, about 80 by 2005, about 100 by 2007, and ends about 131 at the right edge, with a zig-zag pattern of quarterly peaks and dips, the largest peaks about 120 to 128 in 2012 to 2014. Series Amazon (orange line, about 2.5 px stroke): about 0 from 1997 to 1999, about 1 to 2 from 2000 to 2003, about 2 to 4 through 2004 to 2008, about 5 to 10 in 2009 to 2010, about 14 in 2011, about 17 to 21 in 2012 to 2013, rising to about 25 in 2014 and ending about 29 at the right edge. Individual values are not printed, so these are estimates. No points are highlighted. A horizontal solid grey baseline runs along 0 across the plot.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF across the whole slide, with a light grey #F2F2F2 band from about 24% to 93% of the height. Clean flat corporate style with no shadows or gradients. Background white #FFFFFF above and below a light grey #F2F2F2 band. Title text near-black #101010 in ProximaNova Regular, large (about 6.9% of slide height, a sans-serif). Subtitle black #000000 in ProximaNova Regular, about 3.5% of slide height. Chart heading in ProximaNova Semibold, about 3.5% of slide height, colour grey #7F7E7B. Axis numbers in ProximaNova Regular about 2.5% of slide height, grey #7F7E7B for the y axis and dark grey #3F3F3F for the x years. Legend labels dark grey #3F3F3F. Accent colours: Amazon orange about #C87137, Wal-Mart dark grey about #636363. Gridlines dotted mid grey about #7F7E7B. Source line grey #7F7E7B in ProximaNova Regular about 2.5% of slide height.

**Text**

When tech is fully adopted, it disappears
Frequency of ‘Railways’ in Google Books
1800 1810 1820 1830 1840 1850 1860 1870 1880 1890 1900 1910 1920 1930 1940 1950 1960 1970 1980 1990 2000
Source: Google

**Layout**

A full-width white slide with the title left-aligned at about 19% of the slide width in the top band, starting around 6% from the top. A full-bleed light grey panel spans the full width from about 24% to 93% of the height and holds a single line chart whose plot area runs from about 19% to 96% of the width. The x-axis year labels sit under the baseline inside the panel. The source line is right-aligned in the bottom white strip at about 94% from the top, ending at the chart's right edge. No slide number or logo is visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart, untitled apart from the panel heading 'Frequency of ‘Railways’ in Google Books'. Y-axis: no tick labels, no axis title, no units; values are unlabelled, so relative heights are estimates only. X-axis: years 1800 to about 2008, labelled every 10 years from 1800 to 2000, no axis title. Single series, a continuous orange line (about 0.4% of slide height thick) with no markers, labelled with no series name and no legend. Gridlines: six horizontal dotted grey lines at equal intervals, with a solid grey baseline at the bottom. Estimated shape: near zero from 1800 to about 1825; slow rise to about 1.5 grid intervals by 1845; a spike to about 2.4 intervals around 1850; a dip near 1855; about 3.2 intervals by 1880; a secondary peak of about 3.7 intervals around 1890; a dip near 1895; a steep climb to the main peak of about 4.6 intervals around 1915; a decline to about 3.0 intervals around 1930; a plateau near 2.6 intervals from 1950 to 1965; a steady fall to about 1.2 intervals by 1990; a flattening near 0.9 intervals from 2000 to 2008 with a small uptick at the end. No highlighted or annotated points.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF, with a light grey #F2F2F2 band behind the chart.

Steel
Frequency of ‘Steel’ in Google Books
1800 1810 1820 1830 1840 1850 1860 1870 1880 1890 1900 1910 1920 1930 1940 1950 1960 1970 1980 1990 2000

**Layout**

Single-column slide with a white header band holding the title at upper left, and a full-width light grey (#F2F2F2) chart band beginning at about 24% of slide height and ending at about 93%. The title and chart subtitle share a left edge at about 19.5% of slide width, and the chart plot runs from about 19.4% to 96% of width. The chart's year labels sit on a baseline below the plot, and the source line is right-aligned at the bottom right beneath the grey band, with a margin of about 4% from the right edge. No slide number or footer is visible.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Type: line chart. Title: 'Frequency of ‘Steel’ in Google Books', printed above the plot. X axis: years 1800 to about 2008, labelled every 10 years from 1800 to 2000, unit years. Y axis: no axis labels, unit unstated, values not printed; relative frequency only. Series: one series, a single orange line about 4 px thick in #D07A35, drawn as a monthly-resolution curve. Approximate shape: about 1800 to 1840 low and nearly flat; rising from about 1850; a sharp climb from about 1895; first peak about 1918 to 1920; dip to a trough about 1932; second, higher peak about 1943 to 1944 (highest point on the chart); decline through the 1950s; a shoulder around 1970 to 1985; then a steady fall to a low at about 2008. Values are not printed, so the peak and trough values are estimates only: the 1944 peak is about the top of the plot and the 1800 start is about one-tenth of that height. Gridlines: horizontal dotted grey lines, about 10 of them, evenly spaced, with no labels. Baseline: solid mid-grey horizontal axis line at the bottom of the plot. No legend. No highlighted points.

Background: Two-part solid background: white #FFFFFF across the top and bottom, with a light grey #F2F2F2 band across the full width between about 24% and 93% of slide height. Clean minimalist corporate look. White header area; light grey #F2F2F2 chart band behind the chart; title in black #101010 Proxima Nova Regular, sans-serif, regular weight, title size about 6.9% of slide height. Chart subtitle in grey #7F7E7B Proxima Nova Semibold, about 3.5% of slide height. Axis year labels in dark grey #4B4B4B Proxima Nova Regular, about 2.5% of slide height. Source line in grey #7F7E7B Proxima Nova Regular, about 2.5% of slide height. Accent colour is the orange line #D07A35. Gridlines are dotted mid-grey, thin, about 1 px. Baseline is a solid grey line about 2 px thick. No shadows, gradients, rounded corners or borders.
